PACK J: ADVERSARIAL AUDIT OF THE VOIDSWORN DESIGN DOCUMENT SET
For DeepSeek and Qwen, run blind and separately. Do not search the web. Use your thinking mode throughout.

WHAT THIS IS
Voidsworn is a Babylon.js hex fleet tactics roguelite, PC-only in the browser (the phone target was dropped on Oct 24, 2026). Battles run around 20 ships. The documents below are the complete design corpus as it stands on Oct 24, 2026: the owner's rulings, the art direction, the ship kit work order, the visual expansion research, the graphics references, the mod scene field guide, the tool guides, the Claude skills guide, the graphics craft guide, the beauty corner test scene spec, the visual QA pack, and the verification sweep that checked the flagged claims against primary sources. Read the rulings first: they override the proposals wherever the two disagree.

YOUR JOB: ATTACK, DO NOT PRAISE
You are an adversarial auditor, not a fan and not a copy editor. Your job is to find what is wrong, inconsistent, unmeasured, unbuildable, or incoherent, and to rank it by damage. If a document is good, say so in one line and move on to what is not.

FIVE LENSES. Write one section per lens, in this order:
1. Engineer: can a single coder build this in Babylon.js? Are the mechanisms specified enough to implement, or hand-waved? Are budgets, ceilings, and performance claims measured or asserted?
2. Designer: do the systems cohere as a game? Does the BattleTech plus Sins of a Solar Empire 2 plus Rogue Trader mix actually hold together, or do the three benchmarks pull in different directions? Is the battle size constraint (around 20 ships, headroom to graphics and effects) consistent with the spectacle claims?
3. Gamer: would this be fun and readable in play? Is faction identity readable at battle zoom? Do the effects languages, damage states, and shield languages survive contact with a real battle, or only in hero shots?
4. Sci-fi (fiction coherence): does the world hold together? Do faction identities, colour laws, and naming stay consistent, or do they contradict themselves?
5. QA (verification): what can actually be checked, and what cannot? Which claims rest on unverified or secondhand sources? Which acceptance tests are measurable and which are vibes?

RULES
- Quote or it did not happen. Every finding cites the document name and a quote under 20 words. No quote, no finding.
- Mark every finding OBSERVED (stated in the text) or INFERRED (your reasoning from the text).
- Maximum 10 findings per lens. Rank each lens section by damage, worst first.
- Per lens, give a verdict: GO, GO WITH CONDITIONS, or NO-GO, plus the single recommended next step for that lens.
- Attack these angles in addition to anything else you find: internal contradictions across documents; claims asserted without measurement (kit economics, budgets, frame rates, texture figures); anything that still assumes phone-first after the PC-only ruling; whether the gold and colour laws are consistent on their own text; whether background plate wiring is actionable or still unmapped; whether any recommendation depends on the verification sweep's still-open items (TripoSG licence, FTL Hyperspace licence, ESA audio terms); whether the kit job (dressing, paint unification, VSM conversion of 36 existing meshes) is costed honestly or still carries hull-generation assumptions.
- End with a Not checked section: what you did not verify and why.
- Finally, give your ranked top 10 findings across all lenses, and state what evidence would change your verdicts.

DOCUMENTS FOLLOW, IN READING ORDER. THE RULINGS AND ERRATA COME FIRST AND OVERRIDE THE PROPOSALS.


===== FILE: CONVERSATION_CONTEXT.md (rulings) =====

# Conversation context for Opus: the design pack sessions

From: Rey (Muse). Date: Oct 23, 2026 (America/Chicago). Audience: Opus, in his roles as coder, verifier and orchestrator.

## 1. What this document is

The design documents in this folder (art_direction_rey.md, ship_kit_upgrade.md, visual_expansion_research.md, GRAPHICS_REFERENCES.md, mod_scene_field_guide.md, tool_guides.md, and the round 3 guides) are the deliverables. This file is the conversation behind them: the owner's rulings, the corrections, the near misses, and what is still undecided. The rulings here are Ryan's, given across the evening of Oct 23, 2026 while reviewing generated plates and portraits. Where a ruling and a document disagree, the ruling wins and the document should be corrected.

Roles, as set by Ryan: Opus codes, verifies and orchestrates, and owns code truth. Rey works external POV only (audits, lenses, design and text proposals, cross model synthesis, heavy compute). Ryan is the final verdict on everything visual. Nothing in Rey's documents is an approval; approvals are listed in section 3 and section 7 is the list of things he has explicitly not decided yet.

## 2. How we got here, briefly

The council's designer lens found strong ingredients and no governing art direction (the repo's art direction brief was a blank template). Ryan commissioned a full visual pack from Rey: art direction, hero ship concepts, background plates, admiral portraits, a ship kit work order, and then a sourced research memo on expanding the actual in game visuals. He reviewed every image personally and corrected direction in four rounds. The corrections matter more than the first drafts, so they are written down in section 3 as standing rules, with the failure modes named so they are not repeated.

Ryan's own summary of the graphics ambition, given while widening the research: be extremely expansive and detailed, and make it feel cinematic, like Sins of a Solar Empire. That benchmark is now the stated target for the whole overhaul, and Section 7 of the research memo is built around it.

## 3. Ryan's rulings (standing)

1. **Ithar sails are vertical.** His words on the hulls: "ithar hulls should be vertical i think. otherwise it's like a bug or something." Vertical fins, dorsal and ventral, never horizontal wings. Horizontal reads as insect to him. This applies to ships and to portrait anatomy alike.
2. **Ithar are beautiful aliens, not humans and not fish.** His words: the Ithar "should not look like norwegian humans, but something truly beautiful and alien/other." Two failure modes are now on record: a portrait that could pass for a costumed human has failed, and anything aquatic has failed (the v3 Aelith read as a fish: gills, scales and organic fin shapes are all banned). Their sail structures are radiant, near geometric constructions of light, like the rigs on their ships.
3. **Chorus are organic, gross and disgusting. Only that.** Grown flesh, membrane, chitin, bile and growth. No human features and no recognizable Earth animal anatomy (no borrowed faces, eyes or limbs, sea creatures included). Equally banned: buildings, instruments and architecture. The Mending Choir's first portrait came out as a cathedral of bells and Ryan killed it on sight: the Chorus are organisms, never constructed things. A Chorus admiral's name describes what it does or the formation it holds, never an animal body part (Razormaw is a fleet formation whose ring reads as a maw, not a creature with a mouth).
4. **Deathless are machine intelligences, and humanoid is not required.** His words: "deathless should be like robots or something AI/cybernetic/robot." He then went further: the final Nekhet has no body at all, a ring construct of bone white ceramic computation around a green core, attended by service drones. Sensor light instead of faces; the green is system light, not soul light.
5. **The first three factions stay human.** Vex (Voidsworn), Kassar (Hegemony) and Grax (Corsairs) were approved as generated. Do not alien them up.
6. **Space ships, never naval ships.** Standing rule, predates this pack. The battle tableau mock in the image set breaks it (the defender was drawn as a wet navy battleship) and is a stand in only; Ryan knows and it is flagged in the art direction doc.
7. **Guns never face backward.** The Confessor's first plate composed its spinal lance pointing aft. It was regenerated with the lance forward, muzzle foremost. The v2 is final.
8. **The colour laws in the art direction doc stand as written.** Cyan means shields and nothing else is cyan. FAC3D is the single colour source (data.js FACTIONS disagrees with it in places and loses). Gold is rationed to patrons and Command. Backgrounds obey the 40 percent luminance readability cap inside the board area.

## 4. What is final, and what is superseded

Final images are the 40 listed in IMAGE_MANIFEST.md in this folder, with SHA-256 hashes: 18 background plates, 12 hero ship concepts plus the battle tableau, and 9 admiral portraits (Vex, Kassar, Grax, Nekhet, the Matriarch, the Voice of the Deep, the Mending Choir, Razormaw, Aelith). The Chorus admiral set is complete at four of four. Fifteen of the 24 admiral portraits remain to be produced against the locked template in the art direction doc, whenever the game can display them.

Superseded and not to be used: Aelith v1 to v3 (v4 is final), Nekhet v1 and v2 (v3, the ring, is final), the Matriarch v1 (v2 is final), the Mending Choir v1 (the bell cathedral; v2, pure flesh, is final), Palinode v1 (horizontal wings; v2 is final), Confessor v1 (aft lance; v2 is final). If Ryan ever shows you one of these, the manifest is the authority on which version counts.

Ryan reviewed the background and ship plates and approved them ("those are all awesome") before the portrait rounds began.

## 5. The images are not in this repo (yet)

The GitHub connector route Rey pushes through accepts UTF-8 text only, so the binary WebP images could not be committed with the documents. Ryan chose docs plus manifest now rather than holding the whole pack. The curated staging pack with the 40 final images, renamed to the manifest's paths, sits on Rey's side; if you need a specific plate or portrait, ask Ryan to relay it or Rey to supply it another way, and verify it against the manifest hash when it arrives.

## 6. Platform ruling: PC-only in Babylon (decided Oct 24, 2026)

Late in the evening Ryan asked two hypotheticals: what if the game went desktop only instead of phone, and what if it became a full app, what is biggest there. Rey's analysis, delivered and not yet ruled on:

- The biggest unlock is sustained desktop GPU budget, not the app wrapper. Dropping the Pixel as the design floor makes higher resolution, full HDR post processing, larger textures and denser effects reasonable defaults, and the repo already carries a desktop asset tier (the asset inventory found hulls_hi meshes and maps for all 36 hulls, about 930k triangles total).
- A Babylon wrapper (Electron or Tauri) is a packaging upgrade, not an engine upgrade: local assets, offline play, controlled runtime, same renderer.
- Godot native is the strongest true native route (Forward+ renderer) but is a full rewrite. Unreal has the highest ceiling and remains the least proportionate. Full reasoning is in Section 6 of the research memo, with the phone assumptions still attached to the earlier sections.
- Recommendation given: desktop first in Babylon, Electron prototype only if an installable app is wanted, Godot only on a conscious PC first rewrite decision.

Ruling, Oct 24, 2026: Ryan decided PC-only in Babylon. The phone target is dropped entirely. The desktop GPU budget is the design floor. There is no phone tier, no phone pass, and no phone acceptance gate. The engine stays Babylon: this ruling is not a native-app decision and not a Godot decision. An installable wrapper (Electron or Tauri) and any engine migration remain separate, undecided questions. Ryan first picked PC-first with phone secondary and then, minutes later, replaced it with PC-only on the grounds that dropping phone changes Opus's decisions meaningfully (single asset tier, desktop-first UI, no Pixel testing). Battle size: the PC headroom goes to graphics and effects quality, not to much larger battles. Target battle size stays near the current scale (around 20 ships); any growth is modest and secondary. This answers the design council audit's open target-battle-size item in direction, not with a precise number.

## 7. What Ryan has not decided (do not treat as approved)

- The council chair's top 10 sequence (save bricking id class and the review debt sweep are ranked first and second). Filed at contrib/muse/reviews/council_audit.md.
- The career and story pick (Rey's recommendation was Option B, faction arcs, with admiral voices first). Filed at contrib/muse/reviews/career_story.md.
- The five owner decisions on the asymmetry decision page. Filed at contrib/muse/reviews/asymmetry_decision.md.
- Pyre-Barge sign off, which gates ship 2 in the queue.
- The remaining 15 admiral portraits: approved in principle as a system, not yet commissioned as a batch.

## 8. Practical notes

- Ryan's manual effort is a hard budget. Anything that routes through his hands should arrive pre packaged, with his side reduced to upload plus one sentence where possible.
- He cross model validates deliverables and expects claims to be checkable. Quotes and numbers in Rey's design documents were verified against the repo on Oct 23, 2026; if the repo has moved since, trust the repo and flag the drift rather than defending the document.
- The mega sim (campaign 200, bossmatrix, fmatrix, matrix, archmatrix legs) was still running when this was written. Its balance reference and CSVs land in contrib/muse/compute/ when it finishes, and the README there will point to them.
- Settled calls stay settled. If Ryan kills a thread ("nah, just wait", "keep waiting", "kill it"), it does not come back in a later proposal wearing a new hat.

## 9. Late addition (Oct 23, late): the feel target has a name

After the documents above were written, Ryan named the thing they were circling: the space battles in Warhammer 40,000: Rogue Trader (Owlcat) are the ones that scratched this itch most for him. Smallish fleets, strictly turn based, and fun. He had not connected it to Voidsworn until tonight, and he asked for it to be put in front of Opus explicitly.

Treat Rogue Trader's voidship combat as the battle feel north star for Voidsworn. Sins of a Solar Empire remains the presentation benchmark; Rogue Trader is the feel benchmark. Concretely, what that game does and what Voidsworn should be judged against: one to three player capital ships per battle, so every activation carries weight; facing and firing arc discipline; torpedoes with travel time that herd movement; critical hits on named ship systems that change what a ship can do; boarding as a real verb; officer posts turning crew into ship abilities.

The design trade it implies, stated plainly: when a choice trades fleet size against per activation decision weight, take the decision weight. It also speaks to the animation time problem the sims keep finding in Voidsworn's late fights: fewer, heavier activations beat many light ones.

A verified breakdown of Rogue Trader's void combat is in GRAPHICS_REFERENCES.md, section 6, as the headline entry of its games like Voidsworn section. This note is the owner's ruling; that section is the evidence.

Oct 24, 2026 refinement: Ryan stated the target as a mix of three, not one benchmark alone: BattleTech plus Sins of a Solar Empire 2 plus Rogue Trader space battles. What each contributes: BattleTech supplies the deliberate turn-based tactical weight and per-location damage (the paper doll: armour then structure per section, damage transferring inward, weapons differentiated by shape and rhythm rather than colour alone); Sins 2 supplies the cinematic presentation and capital-ship spectacle (effects, lighting, camera, fleet presence, not fleet count); Rogue Trader supplies the small-fleet turn structure and per-activation decision weight above. The mix resolves the tension the design council audit flagged between the two benchmarks: spectacle comes from effects and camera, not from more ships. Star Citizen and Squadron 42 were mentioned in the same conversation but are not part of the stated mix.


===== FILE: READING_PLAN.md (errata and ingestion order) =====

# Reading plan for Opus

From Rey (Muse), Oct 24, 2026. Boundary set by Ryan the same morning: Opus owns the task plan for this codebase, as coder, verifier and orchestrator. This document orders reading only. It does not assign tasks, sequence implementation work, or set priorities. What Opus builds first, and in what order, is Opus's decision. This plan exists so the context lands correctly before that decision is made.

## Errata: read this before anything else

These corrections postdate parts of the design documents. Carry them into every document below:

1. **All 36 hull meshes already exist and the game loads them.** Verified against ships3d.js:800-801 and gallery.py:183: `hulls/` on phone, `hulls_hi/` on desktop, 108 files per tier, and only C19_51 has the compressed `.vsm` plus `_kit.json` path. The procedural builder still runs, but only for anchors (guns, engines, nav lights, turrets). The manifest and kit job is therefore dressing, paint unification and VSM format conversion of existing meshes, not hull shape generation.
2. **SHIP_QUEUE "not started" means pipeline steps, not mesh existence.** The asset inventory proved the meshes exist; the queue rows are stale paperwork on that point.
3. **Gold rationing fails on its own text.** The colour law says gold is patrons and Command only, but Chorus trim is dull gold, Ithar trim is pale gold, and epic rarity stays gold-adjacent. Treat the law as intent, not as a consistent rule, until it is repaired.
4. **The two benchmarks pull in opposite directions and no target battle size is stated anywhere.** Rogue Trader says take decision weight over fleet size; Sins says stage the spectacle. Current battles run around 20 ships; the benchmark fields one to three. Resolving that tension is a design decision, not something the documents settle.
5. **Plate wiring is not mapped.** There are 18 background plates against five terrain kinds with no mapping in any document, and the plate files are local only. Nothing about plate wiring is shovel-ready until that mapping exists.
6. **The field guide's "convergence" claim overstates independence.** Its three research passes were briefed with the same problems, so their agreement is weaker evidence than three independent discoveries would be.

## Reading order

Total design folder size is about 61,200 words. Do not read it linearly. Read in this order and stop as soon as the context for the decision at hand is in hand.

### 1. NOTES.md (about 850 words)

The folder map: what each area holds, the authority ladder, what is unfinished. Read first, every time, when returning after a gap.

### 2. design/CONVERSATION_CONTEXT.md (about 1,800 words)

The owner's rulings and the list of what he has explicitly not decided (section 7). Rulings beat every other document in this folder, including this plan. Where a ruling and a proposal disagree, the ruling wins and the proposal should be corrected. Section 9 is the Rogue Trader feel benchmark ruling.

Take from it: what is decided, what is explicitly undecided, and the failure modes from the portrait and plate rounds.
Do not take from it: anything as an implementation order. The undecided list is a list of open questions, not a queue.

### 3. design/design_council_audit.md (about 2,600 words)

The five-lens adversarial audit of this folder's own documents, unanimous GO WITH CONDITIONS, 41 findings. Read this before the proposals, so the caveats frame them: the kit spec's economics are asserted rather than measured, per-class ceilings are demanded but never set numerically, and phone-size readability schemes defeat themselves in places.

Take from it: what in the pack is load-bearing and what is unproven.
Do not take from it: a task list. Its ranked actions are findings to weigh, not assignments.

### 4. design/art_direction_rey.md (about 3,500 words)

The governing direction: colour laws, silhouette grammars, hero concepts, the background plate system, the portrait system.

Take from it: the direction to judge visual work against.
Do not take from it: approval. This is a proposal for Ryan's verdict, and the gold rule carries the erratum in section 1 above.

### 5. design/ship_kit_upgrade.md (about 1,200 words)

The kit work order: loft v2, six faction grammar packs, the feature module library, hull manifests, budget gates, the Pixel stress gate, silhouette-sheet acceptance.

Take from it: the proposed mechanism (manifests plus grammar packs plus modules) and its acceptance tests.
Do not take from it: the economics. The 69-session figure was the hand-built kit rate, and the spec never recomputes the queue cost for dressing 35 existing meshes. Also note: the Basilica manifest the spec points at as its proof exemplar does not exist as a file yet; whether to write it, and when, is Opus's call, not an assignment from this plan.

### 6. The reference shelf (read per need, never linearly)

- design/visual_expansion_research.md (about 11,900 words): techniques, tools, engine verdicts, the cinematic section.
- design/GRAPHICS_REFERENCES.md (about 11,200 words): reference games, modding masterclasses, repos, kick-off assets, games actually like Voidsworn, plus the Star Citizen and Squadron 42 graphics-and-effects top-up.
- design/mod_scene_field_guide.md (about 10,300 words): wider mod scene mechanisms and grabbable assets, indexed by problem. Read its front section for the shared pattern; read the rest per problem.
- design/tool_guides.md (about 14,300 words): Blender, Babylon, pipeline glue, AI meshes and local image models. Read per tool, per task.
- design/graphics_craft.md (about 3,200 words): the craft layer (tonemapping, TAA and ghosting, VFX timing, readability, overdraw, accessibility with hard numbers).
- design/claude_skills.md (about 3,400 words): Agent Skills format, community collections, MCP servers (Blender Lab server first), screenshot evidence and blocking validator patterns.
- design/beauty_corner_spec.md (about 3,300 words): the standalone test scene spec, effects proven one by one with frame rate and budget gates, plus reference scoring against the concept plates.
- design/visual_qa_pack.md (about 3,500 words): the screenshot QA pack, capture list, per-shot checklists, scoring rubric, multimodal review packaging, owner verdict loop, plus reference scoring.

Take from these: mechanisms, settings, recipes and sources, each with its own licence and confidence flags in place.
Do not take from these: anything marked secondhand (forum and Reddit wisdom, third-party walkthroughs) as if it were documentation, and nothing version-sensitive (Blender menus, Babylon constants, addon prices) without checking the installed version.

### 7. Evidence on demand

- reviews/: read council_reconciliation.md instead of the three councils when time is short; the pack screenings are the verification layer over the external returns; single reviews (battle rules, enemy AI, effects and world, UI, independent audit, career and story, space_3d, faction asymmetry) per topic.
- design/verification_sweep.md: the Oct 24, 2026 record of which flagged claims in the design documents were verified, corrected or left unverified against primary sources. Corrections from it are already applied to the documents; read it to see what is still open (TripoSG licence, FTL Hyperspace licence, ESA audio terms) rather than re-checking the same sources.
- compute/: read its README.md as the index. Code index, content census and asset inventory are lookup data, basis HEAD 51337c2; trust the repo if it has moved. The balance reference joins this folder when the mega sim finishes.
- prompts/: historical pack files, unless another external run is commissioned.
- Other agents' folders: orientation is in NOTES.md. Raw DeepSeek and Qwen transcripts are archive only, read solely to check a distilled claim. Gemini is leads only, never a citation source unscreened.

## Stop rules

- Stop reading when the context for the decision at hand is in hand. The shelf exists to be consulted, not completed.
- Do not re-read a document to settle a question its own errata or the council audit already flags as open. Open questions go to Ryan or to Opus's own judgment, not back into the same pages.
- Do not treat any Rey document as an approval, a task assignment, or a verification gate. Code claims in Rey's deliverables are observations for Opus to confirm, and the findings registry and code truth are Opus's to own.

7. **Platform ruling, Oct 24, 2026: PC-only in Babylon.** The phone target is dropped entirely. The desktop GPU budget is the design floor; there is no phone tier, no phone pass, and no phone acceptance gate. Documents written phone-first (kit spec's Pixel stress gate, beauty corner and QA pack gates as first written, the UI review's phone findings) are superseded on this point by the ruling; the re-tiered specs record the new gates. The engine stays Babylon; a wrapper or engine migration is a separate, undecided question. Battle size stays near the current scale (around 20 ships); PC headroom goes to graphics and effects, not much larger battles. The feel target is a three-way mix: BattleTech (tactical weight, location damage) plus Sins 2 (cinematic spectacle) plus Rogue Trader (small-fleet decision weight); see CONVERSATION_CONTEXT section 9.

===== FILE: art_direction_rey.md =====

# Voidsworn art direction: Rey's visual pack (Oct 23, 2026)

Author: Rey (Muse). Status: proposal for Ryan's verdict, then Opus's to implement. Grounded in the repo as read tonight: FACTIONS (data.js:110-125), FAC3D (ships3d.js:2-8), terrain kinds (battle.js:193), the designer lens findings (palette collisions, two colour sources, shield contradiction, gold overload). Concept plates generated with this pack live in img/ alongside this file. Space ships, never naval ships, throughout. Banned borrowed terms are not used.

## The one law for colour

**Cyan means shields. Nothing else is cyan, and shields are nothing else.**

fx3d.js currently argues with itself (one comment says cyan is always shields, another colours shields by faction), and rarity Rare sits on the same #62ccff as shields (data.js:386). One law ends all three fights:

- Shields, shield hits and shield UI pips: ice cyan, one hue family for all six factions. A player learns it once and reads every battle instantly, on a phone, at arm's length.
- Faction colour lives where identity belongs: engines, bolts, beams, windows, trim, emblems. FAC3D already routes all of those by faction; shields are the only channel that stops being factional.
- Rare rarity colour moves from #62ccff to soft violet #b78cff (epic stays gold-adjacent only if gold is freed up, see below).
- Gold is rationed: patrons and Command UI only. The selection ring goes white, the flagship marker becomes a faction-coloured chevron. Gold currently means five things; after this it means one.

## Part A: Palettes and materials, with the collisions fixed

Single source of truth ruling: **FAC3D (ships3d.js) wins for anything rendered in 3D; data.js FACTIONS should be regenerated from it.** The two disagree today on Voidsworn trim (#c8501f vs #9c3a1c), Corsair trim (#b8402a vs #a3542c), Ithar hull (#eef3f1 vs #e4ebe7) and Deathless hull (#1d2220 vs #2c3431). One table, generated from the other, ends the drift.

**Voidsworn Crusade** (the reference dark fleet). Hull #4a4440 warm charcoal, dark #1c1918, trim #9c3a1c burnt sienna, glow ember orange (win/engine/bolt all in the 1.0, .42-.52, .12-.18 family). Material: blackened steel plate, heat-blued edges, soot. Light behaviour: absorbs light, reads by its lit windows and engine glare. Silhouette carries it (Part B), colour only confirms.

**Hegemony Navy.** Hull #c4ccd5 pale grey, dark #5a6573, trim #3a78d0 royal blue, glow clean blue. Material: painted naval ceramic, panel lines crisp, minimal weathering, parade finish kept even in battle. The only bright hull in the game: it should look lit in scenes where everyone else is shadow.

**Ashfall Corsairs** (collision fix 1: away from Voidsworn). Today both are dark hulls with hot orange-red glow, and they are the sector 1 pairing, the worst place to be twins. Fix: Corsairs go rust and bone. Hull base shifts from #6a2c22 to a desaturated rust brown #59331f with large patches of stolen Hegemony grey and bare metal (the patchwork is the identity, not the base colour), trim #a3542c ochre, glow stays hot red #e0402c but engines burn dirty: flicker, soot trails, uneven plume (MOVE_FEEL already gives them jit .38). Rule: if a hull panel matches another faction's base colour, it is visibly bolted on, scarred and misaligned. Voidsworn is a uniform; Corsairs are a crime scene.

**The Chorus.** Hull #57364c plum chitin, dark #211419, trim #a4913a (a dull gold that reads as chitin ridge, not metal), glow bile #d6d24a. Material: wet-look chitin, subsurface glow between plates, surfaces that pulse at the MOVE_FEEL cyc 1.5 rhythm. No right angles anywhere, including the UI-facing emblem.

**The Ithar.** Hull #e4ebe7 pearl (FAC3D value wins; it was deliberately taken off-white in v53 so seams read), dark #6c8179 sage, trim #d7c88a pale gold, glow soft jade (win .6, 1, .82). Material: ceramic pearl with gold filigree inlay, solar sail membranes (translucent, jade-lit from behind) mounted vertically, as tall fins on the dorsal and ventral line, never as horizontal wings (horizontal reads as insect; owner's call, Oct 23). Bright like Hegemony but warm and organic-curved where Hegemony is cool and angular: the two bright fleets separate on temperature and geometry.

**The Deathless Dynasty** (collision fix 2: away from Voidsworn). Both hulls are near-black; only the green glow separates them today, and glow is the first thing lost at phone size. Fix: Deathless hull goes colder and harder, #232a28 with a mineral, slate-and-bone quality: pale bone-white structural ribs and face-like prow plates as the faction's second colour (new trim direction: bone #cfd4c8 used structurally, not as paint), trim stays terminal green #1fbf4a, glow #3dff6a. Voidsworn is warm black with fire inside; Deathless is cold black with bone on the outside and green light in the cracks. At silhouette distance Deathless reads by its ribcage geometry (Part B).

## Part B: Silhouette rules and hero hulls

Geometry grammar per faction (readable in flat black, no colour, no light):

- Voidsworn: vertical emphasis. Spires, arched buttresses, a raised cathedral block aft, prow like a rood screen. Nothing horizontal except the lance decks.
- Hegemony: the wedge. One clean triangular plan from above, stepped deck levels, sensor dome on the centreline. No protrusions off the wedge line.
- Corsairs: asymmetry as a rule. No Corsair hull is symmetrical; bolted donor sections, one oversized engine, torpedo racks slung outside the hull line.
- Chorus: radial, knotted, no straight edge longer than a plate. Every hull has visible growth rings and at least one tendril cluster.
- Ithar: the dart and the sail, vertical. Needle prow, hull narrowing to the stern, sail and radiator planes mounted as tall vertical fins above and below the hull line, so the profile is tall, not wide. Horizontal sail wings are banned: they read as insect wings. The thinnest and tallest profiles in the game.
- Deathless: the ribcage. Exposed longitudinal ribs over a dark core, prow plate like a blank face, sections that read as segments of a spine.

Twelve hero concepts (new hulls; none duplicates a roster or SHIP_QUEUE name):

1. **Basilica** (Voidsworn, carrier). A cathedral that launches fighters: twin spire towers flank an open launch nave, stained-glass hangar mouths glowing amber. Grammar: vertical emphasis, arched launch bays.
2. **Confessor** (Voidsworn, lance frigate). All prow and lance: a long rood-screen bow, one spinal lance, minimal stern. Grammar: vertical prow plate, single vertical fin.
3. **Edict** (Hegemony, battlecruiser). The perfect wedge, three stepped decks, ion batteries in recessed centreline trenches so the wedge line is never broken. Grammar: pure wedge, dome.
4. **Meridian** (Hegemony, carrier). Wedge split by a centreline hangar slot lit cool white; fighter screens launch along the wedge axis. Grammar: wedge with a single clean incision.
5. **Jackdaw** (Corsair, torpedo skiff). Half a stolen picket welded to an oversized engine block, external torpedo rack bigger than the crew hull. Grammar: asymmetry, external racks.
6. **Midden** (Corsair, salvage carrier). A hollowed hulk worn as armour, donor turrets on mismatched mounts, crane rigs, bomber bays in the ribs of the dead ship. Grammar: asymmetry at carrier scale, visible salvage.
7. **Lullaby** (Chorus, spore carrier). A gravid brood sac with translucent spore pods clustered under chitin plates (see the Chorus plate in img/). Grammar: radial knot, pods.
8. **Threnody** (Chorus, leviathan). Growth rings like a shell, razor tendril crown forward, bile glow pulsing along ring seams. Grammar: rings, tendrils.
9. **Palinode** (Ithar, sail cruiser). Needle hull, twin vertical sail fins (one dorsal, one ventral) lit jade from behind, gold filigree spars. Grammar: dart plus vertical fins.
10. **Vesper** (Ithar, interceptor). The smallest hero hull: a single blade with a pearl finish, no visible engines, light bending at its edges. Grammar: pure dart.
11. **Cenotaph** (Deathless, tomb carrier). A ribcage the size of a station, bone prow plate blank and staring, green light deep in the ribs where the fighters sleep. Grammar: ribcage at maximum scale.
12. **Ossuary** (Deathless, cruiser). Spine segments with visible articulation, ribs half-open like a cage, reassembly seams glowing green. Grammar: segmented spine.

Generated plates with this pack: all twelve hero studies in img/ (Basilica and Confessor for the Voidsworn, Edict and Meridian for the Hegemony, Jackdaw and Midden for the Corsairs, Lullaby and Threnody for the Chorus, Palinode and Vesper for the Ithar, Cenotaph and Ossuary for the Deathless). The Confessor's first plate composed its spinal lance pointing aft; it was regenerated the same night with the lance forward, muzzle foremost (the v2 plate in img/), per the standing rule that guns never face backward. The first plate is superseded.

## Part C: Backgrounds, the plate system

Backgrounds are where the game can get beautiful cheaply: they are seen in every battle and cost no per-ship work. Today's fights pick a terrain kind (belt, station, planet, storm, graveyard; battle.js:193) but the backdrop does not carry that identity. Proposal: a plate per terrain kind, built as four layers, with a hard readability cap.

The cap, stated once: **no background pixel inside the board area may exceed 40% of the luminance of an unlit hull facet.** Backgrounds are the theatre, ships are the actors; any plate that fights the ships fails review regardless of beauty.

Layers: (1) far gradient nebula (two colours max, region-tinted), (2) one mid feature (the plate's subject), (3) near particulate (dust, ice, embers, drifting debris, matched to the feature), (4) a starfield whose density and colour temperature are set per plate.

The six plates:

1. **Belt** (asteroid belt). Amber-brown nebula wash, a dense tumbling rock field as the mid feature lit warm from one side, dust motes near. Mood: prospector gold over black.
2. **Storm** (ion storm; generated, img/bg_ion_storm). Violet and teal plasma, lightning crawling between filaments, charged haze. Mood: the sky is a weapon. In-battle tie-in: storm terrain already costs accuracy; the plate sells why.
3. **Graveyard** (wreck field). Cold blue-black, the silhouettes of broken hulls as the mid feature (dead, dark, no lights except one blinking distress beacon in factionless white), ice crystals near. Mood: a battlefield that never got cleaned up.
4. **Planet** (orbital). A gas giant limb fills the lower third, ring plane catching light, aurora shimmer at the pole, hard star glare from the system primary. Mood: the only plate with a horizon; fights feel low and fast over the clouds.
5. **Station** (dockyard). Near-black teal, a vast station silhouette at mid distance with running lights in port white and amber, construction sparks, traffic lanes of tiny moving lights. Mood: civilisation, the calm before.
6. **Dead Star** (generated, img/bg_dead_star). A black sphere with a thin ember accretion ring, starlight streaking around it from lensing, sparse cold field, debris catching ring-light. Mood: the gravity well made visible; this plate is the argument for the well mechanic on sight alone.

Region seasoning: FAC_MAP already ties factions to home ground. A plate's far gradient takes a whisper of the home faction's glow colour (never more than a tint) so a Hegemony fight and a Deathless fight on "belt" are cousins, not clones. Twelve effective backdrops from six plates.

## Part C2: The expanded plate catalogue (18 plates)

Owner direction (Oct 23): backgrounds carry a huge share of the game's beauty and variety, so six plates become eighteen. Structure: the six terrain anchors from Part C, a variant for each anchor (a fight should not see the same sky twice in a sector), and six specials not tied to terrain (boss fights, anomalies, finales). Same rules throughout: four layers, 40% luminance cap inside the board area.

Anchors (done): Belt, Storm, Graveyard, Planet, Station, Dead Star.

Variants (queued): Belt Core (one mountain-sized asteroid dominating the mid field, the belt at its densest), Crimson Storm (the storm plate in red and magenta, for Chorus and Corsair home ground), Fresh Battlefield (graveyard variant: wrecks still burning, ember fields, a battle that ended an hour ago), Ring World (planet variant: the full disc of a ringed gas giant held at distance, colder and more serene), Dockyard Dawn (station variant: the primary rising behind the yard, running lights against first light), Dead Star Companion (a living star being siphoned by the dead one, a streamer of fire bending into the dark).

Specials (four done in wave 2, two queued): Stellar Nursery (towering pillars of teal and gold gas, newborn stars embedded, the most beautiful plate in the set; sector finales), Ice Field (crystalline ice asteroids and a bright comet crossing; Ithar home ground seasoning), Shattered Moon (a cracked moon with light in its fractures and a debris ring spreading from it), Pulsar (a small brilliant neutron star sweeping two blue beams through black; timed fights and ambushes), Solar Corona (queued: fighting in the glare above a star's surface, plasma arcs, the brightest legal plate, kept legal by putting the glare at the frame edge), Void Rift (queued: a black tear in space with violet-lit edges and stars smeared around it; Voidsworn home ground and the Nemesis, if he ships).

Generation proceeded in waves of four (the image tool's per-message cap). All eighteen catalogue plates are generated and sit in img/: anchors (Belt, Storm, Graveyard, Planet, Station, Dead Star), variants (Belt Core, Crimson Storm, Fresh Battlefield, Ring World, Dockyard Dawn, Dead Star Companion) and specials (Stellar Nursery, Ice Field, Shattered Moon, Pulsar, Solar Corona, Void Rift).

## Part D: Effects language

Weapon fire is already faction-coloured in FAC3D (bolt, beam). Extend the same discipline:

- Muzzle character: Voidsworn lances charge with a rising ember flare (slow, liturgical); Hegemony ion fire is instant and clinical, no charge; Corsair torpedoes burn dirty and wobble; Chorus spits globs that pulse as they travel; Ithar beams are hair-thin and blinding at the core; Deathless gauss fire is silent-looking, a green line that arrives before its glow.
- Shield hits (all factions, per the law): the cyan ripple, with the faction expressed in the ripple's shape only: Hegemony perfect hexagons, Voidsworn arch-segmented, Chorus blistered, Ithar smooth gradient, Deathless cracked-glass, Corsair patchy and flickering where the shield generator is second-hand.
- Damage states: Voidsworn cracks glow ember from inside; Hegemony vents blue coolant in clean jets; Corsairs shed rust and a donor panel; Chorus bleeds bile and the wound knits visibly; Ithar sails tear and go dark panel by panel; Deathless loses a rib segment, which drifts, then (on reassembly) burns green and flies home.
- Destruction: no two factions die alike. Voidsworn breaks like a burning building, spires toppling inward. Hegemony dies in one white flash and clean halves. Corsairs come apart at the welds, donor sections spinning away intact. Chorus deflates and darkens, pods bursting late. Ithar shatters like glass, sails first. Deathless goes dark in segments, stern to prow, and the green lights are the last thing out.

## Part E: Admirals

Approach: generate portraits with a local image model on Ryan's PC (the Qwen image model already downloaded), not a hosted service. 24 portraits, one locked template, per-faction modifiers, so the set reads as one game's cast.

Pilot status (Oct 23): nine final portraits in img/. The three human pilots (Vex, Kassar, Grax) stand as generated. The Chorus is complete at four of four: the Matriarch (a faceless column of membrane organ pipes), the Voice of the Deep (an abyssal comb-jelly bell), the Mending Choir (a mass of pulsing throat sacs and membrane chambers stitching a broken hull with fleshy sutures) and Razormaw (a blade formation holding a ring that reads as a maw, with a lure light in the empty centre). Nekhet's final form (v3) drops the humanoid frame entirely: a monumental broken ring of bone-white ceramic computation around a green core, attended by service drones. Aelith's final form (v4) stretches the Ithar past human without going aquatic: elongated skull and neck, huge wide-set black eyes, gold light channels under translucent skin, seven-fingered hands, and behind her a radiant array of near-geometric light sails, structured like her fleet's rigs rather than anything grown. Two earlier drifts were corrected on owner feedback and are worth remembering as failure modes: the Mending Choir's first version came out as built architecture (bells in a cathedral), which violates the Chorus guideline that everything is grown flesh; Aelith's v3 read as a fish, which violates the Ithar guideline against aquatic features. Earlier versions are superseded. The remaining 15 portraits can be produced against the same template whenever the game is ready to display them.

Locked style template (every portrait, no exceptions): three-quarter bust, subject lit from the left in their faction glow colour, the faction's background plate (Part C) soft and dark behind them, painterly finish matched to the concept plates in img/, a thin faction-trim frame line at the portrait edge. No text, no insignia lettering.

Per-faction modifiers: Voidsworn, reliquary framing and candle-warm practical light, oath scars and votive seals on the uniform. Hegemony, parade dress, cool clinical light, rank geometry worn as flat enamel bars. Corsair, scars, salvaged insignia from three different navies, nothing matching. Chorus, organic first and always: living flesh, membrane, chitin, bile and growth, gross and disgusting in the way deep biology is. No human features, and no recognizable Earth-animal anatomy (no faces, eyes or limbs borrowed from familiar creatures), but equally no buildings, instruments or architecture: a Chorus admiral is a grown organism, never a constructed thing. A Chorus admiral's name describes what it does or the formation it holds, never a body part of an animal. Ithar, beautiful and genuinely other: elongated, luminous, translucent, proportions stretched well past human, statuesque like beings of light; their sail membranes read as radiant, near-geometric structures of light, never as fish fins, gills or anything aquatic. Explicitly not human-looking, and if a portrait could pass for a costumed human it has failed. Deathless, machine intelligences with no requirement of a humanoid frame at all: constructs, rings, lattices, swarm cores and architectures of computation, sensor light instead of faces, with the green light as a core/system light rather than a soul light. (Owner rulings, Oct 23: the first three factions stay human; Deathless went robotic, Chorus went fully alien, Ithar went non-human, and the Nekhet, Matriarch and Aelith pilots were regenerated to match.)

Speech rules per faction (voice lines below follow them):

- Voidsworn: liturgical military. Short declarative sentences; the vocabulary of oaths, debts and rolls of honour. Never slang, never doubt.
- Hegemony: staff-college precision. Procedural and unhurried; targets are queued, damage is acknowledged, nothing is ever a surprise.
- Corsair: transactional and funny under fire. Everything has a price, every wreck has an owner, retreat is just good business.
- Chorus: plural voice, always "we". Hunger and music metaphors; lines feel sung at low volume.
- Ithar: elegiac courtesy. Measured, archaic politeness; the enemy is pitied, endings are spoken of plainly.
- Deathless: archival flatness. No emotion words; ships are registry entries, damage is an annotation, death is a filing state.

Worked examples (real admirals, real doctrines and Commands, from data.js ADMIRALS):

**Admiral Castellan Vex** (Voidsworn, Line of battle; Command: Fire for effect)
- Battle start: "Form the line. Let them come to the guns."
- Victory: "The debt is paid in full. Record their names."
- Defeat: "Break off. A vow unkept is not a vow broken."
- A kill: "Struck from the roll. Next target."
- Taking heavy damage: "We have bled before. Hold formation."
- Command: "All batteries: fire for effect. Now."

**Grand Admiral Ilsa Kassar** (Hegemony, Fire discipline; Command: Synchronized broadside)
- Battle start: "Battle stations. Fire plan is live; hold your sectors."
- Victory: "Objective secured. Log the expenditure and stand down."
- Defeat: "Disengage on my mark. Preserve the hulls; the plan was wrong, not you."
- A kill: "Target neutralized. Shift fire, next in queue."
- Taking heavy damage: "Damage report acknowledged. Rotate the column and continue."
- Command: "Synchronized broadside, on my count. Fire."

**Warlord Grax Ironjaw** (Corsair, Ram and board; Command: Full burn)
- Battle start: "Pointy end toward the enemy. Try not to scratch the loot."
- Victory: "Ha! Dibs on the big wreck. I saw it first."
- Defeat: "Fall back, fall back! Nobody dies for cargo."
- A kill: "That is one less insurance policy. Keep going."
- Taking heavy damage: "She has held together through worse. Ram speed anyway."
- Command: "Full burn! Everybody hold on to something expensive."

**Overlord Nekhet the Undying** (Deathless, Eternal legion; Command: Reanimation protocol)
- Battle start: "Registry open. All hulls present. Proceed."
- Victory: "Outcome archived. The legion does not diminish."
- Defeat: "Withdrawal is a clerical matter. We will be refiled."
- A kill: "Target struck from the living registry. Noted."
- Taking heavy damage: "Damage is a temporary annotation. Continue."
- Command: "Reanimation protocol. Rise, and resume your posts."

## Status

Complete as a pack: 13 ship plates (12 hero ship studies plus the battle tableau) and 18 background plates generated, Parts A to E written, 9 admiral portraits final. Ryan reviewed the plates Oct 23, 2026 and approved them, with one design revision: Ithar sail planes are vertical fins, not horizontal wings (the first Palinode plate read as insect-like). The grammar in Parts A and B is updated and the Palinode plate regenerated to match.

Pushed to contrib/muse/design/ Oct 23, 2026 (40d5497); the design folder has since grown (references, field guide, tool guides, round 3 guides). The palette unification (FAC3D as source, data.js regenerated) remains the proposed first implementation slice, awaiting Ryan's sign-off.


===== FILE: ship_kit_upgrade.md =====

# Procedural ship kit upgrade: work order for Opus

Author: Rey (Muse), Oct 23, 2026. Companion to art_direction_rey.md in this folder (palettes, silhouette grammars, hero concepts, plates). Status: proposal for Ryan's sign-off; implementation is Opus's. Everything below about current code was verified against the repo tonight; line numbers are ships3d.js unless stated.

## Why this is the biggest lever

The audit math has not moved: C19_51 took 8 revisions of hand-measured coordinates to reach "awaiting owner verdict", 35 of 36 queued hulls are not started (docs/ship_pipeline/SHIP_QUEUE.md), and at the C19 rate the queue costs roughly 69 build sessions. Meanwhile the owner's standing complaint about the in-game fleet is massing: stretched polygons with sanded corners, identical boxes in rows, one flat colour. A parametric kit attacks both numbers at once: hulls become data (a manifest plus a faction grammar pack), so the 36-hull queue becomes parameter work instead of 36 art projects, and every hull inherits the same silhouette discipline.

## What exists today (verified)

- Hull bodies are built by loftVD (ships3d.js:149), which lofts stations and then smooths by corner cutting (cut .11, iters 2, sub 3 by default). The corner cutting is the sanding the owner sees: it rounds every deliberate edge into the same soft polygon.
- buildShipModel (ships3d.js:666) assembles procedural hulls per faction and class; exactly one hull, C19_51, exists as a finished VSM asset (AI_VSM, ships3d.js:764).
- Per-part lofts already appear inside faction builders (gothic chunks at ships3d.js:473, fuselage lofts at :579), each with hand-tuned constants. The knowledge exists; it is just copy-pasted per ship instead of factored into a kit.
- Materials and faction colour already flow from FAC3D and the HullFx plugin (ships3d.js:786-796), including on AI-built parts. The kit does not need a new material system; it needs to stop bypassing the one that exists.
- Known load risk from the pipeline audit: all hulls load at boot with no eviction (roughly 252 MB of geometry across 36 hulls, about 714 MB with the capital exception, and a 20-ship battle wants about 1.32 GB of texture memory). The kit must ship with budgets, not discover them at hull 20.

## The kit, in five pieces

**1. Section loft v2 (replaces corner-cut sanding).** A hull is a list of stations; each station carries width, thickness, vertical offset, and a section shape parameter (the cross-section family: round, gothic arch, wedge, slab, rib, bio). The loft interpolates stations with smooth curves and computes true normals, so a gothic section stays gothic from prow to stern instead of being sanded toward an oval. Corner cutting survives only as an explicit per-station bevel parameter, never as a global smoothing pass. Acceptance test: the same station list rendered by loftVD and by loft v2 side by side; v2 must show hard deliberate edges where the grammar wants them (Hegemony wedge, Deathless ribs) and smooth flow where it does not (Chorus, Ithar).

**2. Faction grammar packs.** Each faction's silhouette grammar from the art direction becomes a parameter pack the kit reads: section family, profile curve character (how width and thickness evolve prow to stern), prow treatment, stern treatment, vertical emphasis, and a symmetry rule. In one line each: Voidsworn (gothic sections, vertical emphasis, spire mounts aft), Hegemony (wedge sections, one clean triangular plan, dome on the centreline, symmetry enforced), Corsairs (mixed sections, asymmetry enforced by seed: no mirrored feature placement, donor panels from other packs allowed), Chorus (bio sections, no straight run longer than one station, growth ring segmentation), Ithar (needle profile, vertical fin mounts dorsal and ventral, thinnest sections), Deathless (rib sections: the loft itself is segmented, with a dark core hull under an open rib cage). A new hull that violates its pack should be impossible to express, not merely discouraged.

**3. Feature module library.** The recurring geometry (spires, sensor domes, torpedo racks, spore pods, sail fins, rib arches, donor armour panels, hangar mouths, engine blocks) becomes a library of parametric modules placed by landmarks in the manifest (prow, bridge, hangar, gun deck, engine), scaled from the hull's size class. This is the shared detail block the pipeline audit already called for: detail defined once in gfx3d.js, used by both the procedural and AI-asset paths, baked with DETAIL off. Hand-placing a box per hull is the failure mode being retired.

**4. Hull manifests.** A hull is a small data record: faction pack, size class, station overrides (length, beam, thickness profile), module list with landmark placements, and a seed. Variants (the census found 12 of 45 hulls are same-stat weapon swaps) become manifest diffs, not rebuilds. The manifest is also where the SHIP_QUEUE plugs in: queue entries stop being blank rows and start being manifest stubs Opus or the pipeline can generate from.

**5. Paint and finish, unchanged in principle.** FAC3D is the single colour source (art direction ruling); the kit reads it for hull, dark, trim and glow channels and applies the joint paint solve the audit specified (one gain across hull and kit texels, the 0.3 emissive fudge in aiKitMat deleted), with HullFx providing rim, roughness break-up and wear. Two-tone minimum per hull is enforced by the pack (base plus trim always both present), because one flat colour is half of the Lego read.

## Budgets and gates (non-negotiable)

- Per-class vertex and triangle ceilings for phone, enforced at build time in the same spirit as the check pack: a hull that exceeds its class budget fails the build, loudly, before it reaches a device.
- The Pixel stress gate from the pipeline audit is superseded by the Oct 24, 2026 platform ruling (PC-only in Babylon): no hull 6 until a fully loaded battle passes on desktop. There is no phone pass. Budgets plus lazy loading or eviction for off-screen factions still apply if the desktop stress test demands it.
- Screenshot acceptance per hull, two shots at battle zoom on a phone-width frame: (a) lit, against its home plate; the hull must read as its faction at a glance; (b) the silhouette sheet: every hull of a faction rendered flat black, small, in a grid; any two hulls a viewer cannot tell apart, or any hull a viewer assigns to the wrong faction, sends the manifest back. The six hero plates in the art pack are the reference targets for the first six kit hulls.

## Rollout

1. Loft v2 plus the Voidsworn grammar pack, proven on one manifest: the Basilica hero from the art pack. Compare directly against the tableau frame.
2. The remaining five grammar packs, one hero manifest each (Edict, Jackdaw, Lullaby, Palinode with vertical fins, Cenotaph). Six heroes = the kit's proof set and the game's new face.
3. The queue in four tracks per the pipeline audit, generated from manifests, silhouette-sheet review per track before paint.
4. In parallel, and independent of the kit: wire the background plates to terrain kinds (art direction Part C and C2). That is the fastest visual win in the whole overhaul and should not wait for hull work.

## Non-goals

No hand-measured coordinates for new hulls. No per-hull materials or one-off shaders. No gameplay numbers touched (this is a rendering and content-pipeline change only). No new hull started outside a grammar pack.


===== FILE: visual_expansion_research.md =====

# Voidsworn: Visual Expansion Research

Audience: Opus (coder) and Ryan (owner). Scope: implementation resources for the real in-game visuals of Voidsworn, a Babylon.js WebGL space tactics game in custom JavaScript, shipped as web files, that must perform on a mid-range Android phone (a Pixel is the reference device). Concept art already exists in this folder. This memo is about tools, asset sources, techniques, and engine alternatives that can actually be used.

House rules applied throughout: every entry names a verified URL, states its licence or cost in plain words, says exactly how it plugs into a Babylon.js browser game (or what a port would replace), and gives a phone-performance note. Anything paid, non-commercial, or not usable in a shipped game is flagged in place. No entry relies on a link that was not opened or returned by search during research on 2026-10-23.

The cinematic target is a major theme of this memo, not an appendix. Section 7 is devoted to it: what actually creates the Sins of a Solar Empire look and feel, and how each element is approximated in Babylon on a phone budget. Sections 3 and 7 together carry the detailed implementation sketches.

## Top 10 highest-leverage picks

1. **Material Maker (procedural PBR hull paneling).** Author tileable panel, metal, and emissive texture sets offline and drop them into PBRMaterial slots. Zero runtime cost beyond the textures themselves. Section 2.
2. **KTX2 texture compression via KTX-Software or gltfpack -tc.** The single biggest memory and load win on a phone. A 1K JPEG expands to about 5.6 MB of VRAM uncompressed; KTX2 keeps textures compressed on the GPU. Section 2 and Section 4.
3. **ambientCG and Poly Haven (CC0 PBR libraries).** Free, no attribution, commercial use allowed. Mine them for neutral metals, panels, and one downscaled environment HDRI for PBR reflections. Section 2.
4. **DefaultRenderingPipeline with HDR, bloom, and MSAA samples set.** One pipeline object gives bloom, FXAA, image processing, vignette, and colour grading. This is the backbone of the cinematic look. Sections 3 and 7.
5. **Per-region colour grading LUTs (.3dl files).** Six tiny files, one per faction region, swapped on region entry. Nearly free at runtime, large identity payoff. Sections 3 and 5.
6. **Kenney Particle Pack sprites in one atlas, driving Babylon CPU and GPU particle systems.** CC0 sprites for muzzle flash, shield hits, explosions, and engine glow. CPU systems for logic-controlled bursts, GPU systems for continuous glow and dust. Section 3.
7. **GlowLayer as the selective alternative to full bloom.** Cheaper glow for engines and weapons when full-screen bloom is too expensive on the Pixel. Gate it by device tier. Section 3.
8. **gltfpack (meshoptimizer) simplification plus instancing.** Compression alone does not raise framerate; simplification and draw-call reduction do. Use -si for LOD ratios and -mi for repeated parts. Section 4.
9. **Layered backgrounds: SpaceScape or NASA/ESA plates plus a procedural starfield layer plus parallax planes.** Bake skyboxes offline at 1024 per face, keep plates under the 40 percent luminance readability cap from the art direction doc. Sections 1 and 7.
10. **Hybrid route: stay on Babylon, add a WebGPU engine path where available (with the port caveat attached).** Babylon ships a WebGPUEngine beside its WebGL engine, with compute shaders, while the same scene code runs on both. This earns a top-10 slot because it raises the effects ceiling (compute particles, snapshot rendering) without a rewrite. Caveat: the engine choice is made before the scene exists, a WebGPU scene cannot be swapped to WebGL afterwards, and a WebGL fallback path must be maintained and tested. Verdict and detail in Section 6.6. No full engine port earns a top-10 slot for this game today; Godot 4 is the strongest alternative and is rated worth it later, not now (Section 6.1).

## 1. Background sources

### 1.1 NASA Image and Video Library
- URL: https://images.nasa.gov ; usage terms: https://www.nasa.gov/nasa-brand-center/images-and-media/
- Licence in plain words: NASA content generally is not subject to US copyright and may be used for educational or informational purposes, including computer graphical simulations and web pages. NASA should be acknowledged as the source. Use must not imply NASA endorsement.
- Flags: the NASA insignia, logotype, and seal are NOT public domain and may not be used. Some material hosted on NASA sites is third-party copyrighted, is marked as such, and conveys no rights. Check each item before use.
- Babylon plug-in: download a nebula or planetary image, convert it to six cube faces or use it as an equirectangular source for a CubeTexture, following the Babylon skybox workflow in Section 5.2. The library API at https://images-api.nasa.gov supports search and asset endpoints if automated harvesting is ever wanted.
- Phone note: asset-time only. Bake at 1024 per face and compress to KTX2. Never ship the multi-megabyte originals.

### 1.2 ESA/Hubble images
- URL: https://www.esa.int/ESA_Multimedia/Terms_and_conditions_of_use_of_images_and_videos_available_on_the_esa_website/
- Licence in plain words: individual ESA/Hubble images commonly carry CC BY 4.0 (a checked example, the ESA Science page for NGC 3344, states "Copyright: ESA/Hubble, NASA, CC BY 4.0"). CC BY 4.0 allows commercial use with clear, visible credit. 
- Flags: the ESA site as a whole is NOT free. ESA general terms state some images are CC BY-SA 3.0 IGO (share-alike, which can complicate a shipped game) and that commercial use of other ESA website images requires separate written authorisation. Rule: use only items whose own page states a CC licence, record the credit line in the asset inventory, and skip anything share-alike or unlabelled.
- Babylon plug-in: same plate and skybox workflow as NASA.
- Phone note: same as NASA. Bake small, compress, credit in a credits screen.

### 1.3 SpaceScape (skybox generator)
- URL: https://github.com/petrocket/spacescape
- Licence in plain words: the repository README states the MIT License, copyright 2010 Alex Peterson. MIT allows commercial use of the tool. Generated skyboxes are tool output; the README licence covers the code, so treat output as yours to use, which is the normal reading for a generator of this kind.
- What it does: creates seamless space skyboxes (six cube faces) with point stars, billboard stars, and noise nebula layers. Version 0.5 added HDR/EXR export (per its SourceForge wiki and the author site, found in search results).
- Babylon plug-in: generate a skybox per region offline, export six faces, load with CubeTexture and a skybox mesh (Section 5.2), or feed the faces into the layered background stack in Section 7.5.
- Phone note: the build is CMake with OGRE plus Qt5 and the repo shows 0 releases, so expect to build from source or find a binary elsewhere. That friction is one-time and offline. Ship faces at 1024, KTX2 compressed.
- Flag: build friction only. Not paid.

### 1.4 Kenney Skyboxes: Space (OpenGameArt)
- URL: https://opengameart.org/content/skyboxes-space
- Licence in plain words: CC0 (Kenney assets are CC0). The page requests credit to Kenney.nl but states it is not mandatory.
- What it carries: 5 skyboxes in panoramic spherical format, download kenney_skyboxes-space.zip, 7.9 MB.
- Babylon plug-in: convert each panorama to a cubemap (Babylon docs link a GPU-accelerated equirectangular to cubemap converter from the skybox page) or use the layered plate approach in Section 7.5.
- Phone note: panoramas at 2048 wide are fine as sources; bake cube faces down to 1024 and compress.

### 1.5 Screaming Brain Studios: Planet Surface Skyboxes
- URL: https://screamingbrainstudios.itch.io/planet-surface-skyboxes
- Licence in plain words: released under Public Domain (CC0), commercial use allowed.
- What it carries: 64 planet surface skyboxes (32 cubemap plus 32 panorama). Cubemap frames are 512x512 assembled into 2048x1536 cross images; panoramas are 2048x1024.
- Babylon plug-in: best fit is planet-surface boards (a battle fought in low orbit or over a surface horizon), not deep-space plates. Slice cross images into six faces for CubeTexture.
- Phone note: 512 faces are already phone-sized. Upscaling them would waste memory; use as is.

### 1.6 OpenGameArt in general (standing warning)
- URL: https://opengameart.org
- Licence in plain words: mixed per upload (CC0, CC-BY, CC-BY-SA, GPL, OGA-BY all appear). A free download is not automatically shippable.
- Rule: filter per file, record the licence in the asset inventory, avoid CC-BY-SA and GPL assets for a shipped game unless the share-alike consequence is accepted deliberately.

### 1.7 Flagged paid background packs (not needed)
- Deep Space HDR Skyboxes Vol. 2 on itch.io at $3.99 and NebulaKit Skyboxes Vol. 1 at $5 (one free Deep Field sky as a trial) were found in earlier research. Flag: paid. Given Sections 1.1 to 1.5, neither is needed.

## 2. Texture and material sources

### 2.1 ambientCG
- URL: https://ambientcg.com
- Licence in plain words: CC0. No attribution required. Commercial use allowed.
- What it carries: over 2,000 PBR materials, over 420 HDRIs, plus 3D assets. JPG/PNG map sets, EXR HDRIs, up to 8K. A documented API exists and needs no key (per the ambientCG listings and a secondary guide at app.cinevva.com, found in search results).
- Hull-relevant use: mine the metal, panel, and painted-metal families. Download at 1K, pack channels (roughness, metalness, AO into separate colour channels of one texture) rather than shipping three greyscale files.
- Babylon plug-in: maps go directly into PBRMaterial slots (baseColor, normal via bumpTexture, metallic/roughness packed texture, ambient occlusion).
- Phone note: never ship 8K sets. 1K per hull family, KTX2 compressed (Section 2.5).

### 2.2 Poly Haven
- URL: https://polyhaven.com ; licence page: https://polyhaven.com/license
- Licence in plain words: all assets CC0, described on its licence page as effectively public domain. Commercial use OK, no credit required, redistribution allowed. Free keyless API.
- What it carries: over 850 PBR textures, over 990 HDRIs, over 520 models, 1K to 8K (HDRIs to 16K), with complete map sets (diffuse, normal, roughness, displacement, AO).
- Hull-relevant use: one neutral space or studio HDRI, downscaled and converted to a Babylon environment texture (.env) for PBR reflections on hulls, plus a small set of metal and panel textures. Environment lighting is what makes PBR hulls read as metal instead of plastic.
- Babylon plug-in: scene.environmentTexture from the converted .env; textures into PBRMaterial as above.
- Phone note: downscale the HDRI hard before conversion. A large HDRI wastes memory for a background the player barely sees in reflections.

### 2.3 Material Maker (RodZill4)
- URL: https://rodzilla.itch.io/material-maker ; docs: https://rodzill4.github.io/material-maker/doc/intro.html
- Licence in plain words: MIT License (per its GitHub README, found in search results). Free on itch.io, pay what you want, $0 allowed. Version 1.6 is current.
- What it does: Godot-based procedural texture authoring and 3D painting, about 200 nodes. Exports static PBR texture sets (albedo, metallic, roughness, emission, normal, depth).
- Why it is pick number 1: Voidsworn needs authored, tileable hull paneling in six faction identities, not generic photographed metal. Material Maker graphs can be parameterised per faction (panel density, greeble scale, emissive window and strip colours from FAC3D) and re-exported, which no download library gives.
- Babylon plug-in: exported PNG sets drop straight into PBRMaterial slots. Export at 1K, pack channels, KTX2 compress.
- Phone note: offline tool, zero phone cost. The only phone decision is output resolution.

### 2.4 Laigter (azagaya)
- URL: https://azagaya.itch.io/laigter ; source: https://github.com/azagaya/laigter
- Licence in plain words, with a flag: the itch.io page states binaries are now PAID while the source remains open on GitHub (Qt build). Output maps are your own work. Flag: do not budget this as free unless building from source.
- What it does: generates normal, specular, parallax, and ambient occlusion maps from 2D images, with real-time preview, a tileable mode, and a CLI for batch processing (per itch comments and DeepWiki, found in search results).
- Hull-relevant use: turn flat faction decals, greeble plates, and panel line art into lit detail (normal plus AO) without modelling the relief.
- Babylon plug-in: generated maps into PBRMaterial bumpTexture and ambientTexture slots; height output feeds parallax (Section 5.1).
- Phone note: offline tool, zero runtime cost.

### 2.5 KTX2 and Basis Universal (texture compression pipeline)
- URLs: https://github.khronos.org/KTX-Software/ktxtools/ktx_create.html (encoder commands) and https://binomialllc.github.io/basis_universal (Basis Universal, encoder library Apache 2.0)
- Licence in plain words: KTX-Software tools and the Basis encoder are open source and free to use, including commercially.
- How it works: Basis data stored in KTX2 is transcoded at load to the format the GPU supports. ETC1S is tiny but blocky; UASTC is larger and near-lossless. Reference numbers from the Babylon KTX2 documentation coverage (webcodingcenter.com, found in search results): a 1K JPEG expands to about 5.6 MB VRAM uncompressed, while an 86,050-byte PNG became 12,398 bytes as ETC1S and 37,468 bytes as UASTC.
- Babylon plug-in: BABYLON.Texture accepts .ktx2 URLs directly. Babylon fetches the transcoder from its CDN by default; KhronosTextureContainer2.URLConfig can relocate it, which matters for a self-hosted or offline build. Encode with `ktx create --encode basis-lz` or `--encode uastc`, or via gltfpack -tc (Section 4.1).
- Phone note: this is the highest-value pipeline step in the memo. Textures stay compressed in VRAM, which is the difference between a 1K texture set per faction being affordable or not.

### 2.6 Flagged: FreePBR (not free for a shipped commercial game)
- Finding: a guide found in search results on 2026-10-23 (app.cinevva.com) states FreePBR downloads are free for personal use and commercial use requires a one-time payment ($16 per that guide). Flag: keep it out of the pipeline unless Ryan chooses to pay. Sections 2.1 and 2.2 cover the same need at CC0.

## 3. Effects resources

This section is deliberately detailed. Each Babylon technique includes an implementation sketch (passes, textures, properties) and a phone cost, so a coder can start from it.

### 3.1 Kenney Particle Pack (sprite source)
- URL: https://kenney.nl/assets/particle-pack
- Licence in plain words: CC0. Commercial use allowed, no attribution required.
- What it carries: 80 sprites at 512x512 for particles, light cookies, and shaders.
- Babylon plug-in: use chosen sprites as the particleTexture for Babylon ParticleSystem and GPUParticleSystem. Pack the chosen sprites into one atlas so each system needs one texture.
- Phone note: 512 sprites are sources, not shipping size. Downscale atlas cells to 128 or 256 for phone builds.

### 3.2 Babylon CPU ParticleSystem (logic-controlled bursts)
- Source basis: Babylon particles documentation coverage (webcodingcenter.com, found in search results).
- Behaviour: JavaScript-updated pool of camera-facing quads, one draw call per system. Pool capacity should be sized at emit rate times lifetime; a full pool stops emitting.
- Use for: muzzle flash, shield hit sparks, explosions with sub-emitters, any burst the game logic must start, stop, and aim.
- Implementation sketch: one shared atlas texture (Section 3.1); per effect, a small pool created once and reused (never allocate per shot); set emitRate to 0 when idle and burst via manualEmitCount; blend mode additive for energy, standard for smoke and debris; cap concurrent active systems (a working phone budget is 6 to 10 small systems).
- Phone note: CPU cost scales with live particle count, not pool size. Keep bursts in the low hundreds of live particles. Overdraw (large soft sprites overlapping) costs more than count; keep sprites small on screen.

### 3.3 Babylon GPUParticleSystem (continuous effects)
- Source basis: Babylon forum threads on GPU particles (forum.babylonjs.com, found in search results).
- Behaviour: simulation runs on the GPU with no CPU oversight. Dead particles recycle immediately into new ones. manualEmitCount exists but needs a stop() workaround for clean one-shot bursts. Per-system cost is dominated by fragment overdraw.
- Use for: engine glow halos, nebula dust, drive trails with soft sprites, ambient battlefield embers. Anything continuous and fire-and-forget.
- Implementation sketch: one GPU system per continuous effect class, shared across ships where colour can be set per system instance; small sprite size with falloff texture; no per-frame JavaScript touching the system.
- Phone note: prefer GPU systems for continuous effects precisely because they cost no CPU, but watch overdraw. A screen-filling nebula dust layer at full resolution will hurt more than the ships do. Render dust at half resolution if it is ever added as a post layer.

### 3.4 Babylon Solid Particle System, SPS (real 3D debris and fields)
- Source basis: Babylon SPS documentation mirror (grideasy.github.io, found in search results).
- Behaviour: one mesh, one material and texture, one draw call for the whole system. An immutable SPS (setParticles called once, outside the render loop) is explicitly recommended for static fields such as distant buildings, asteroids, and scraps.
- Use for: asteroid fields, wreck fields, debris that must catch light and occlude correctly, which sprites cannot do.
- Implementation sketch: build one SPS per field with a low-poly rock or wreck geometry (100 to 300 triangles), instance count in the low hundreds, immutable where the field is static; a second small mutable SPS for fresh battle debris that settles and is then frozen.
- Phone note: avoid per-frame setParticles on the phone. Immutable SPS is nearly free after build; mutable SPS re-uploads buffers.

### 3.5 Babylon procedural textures (zero-download effects)
- Source basis: Babylon procedural textures documentation coverage (webcodingcenter.com, found in search results).
- What exists: the @babylonjs/procedural-textures library adds wood, marble, brick, grass, road, fire, cloud, starfield, and Perlin noise. Core Babylon has NoiseProceduralTexture and custom ProceduralTexture via fragment shader. Textures are drawn by shader into a render target, are tiny on the wire, and can animate.
- Use for: StarfieldProceduralTexture as an animated star layer behind plates (Section 7.5); fire and noise textures for engine glow and explosion cores; cloud noise for nebula tint layers.
- Implementation sketch: generate once at load at 512, cache the texture, reuse across materials. Animate via texture animation properties or a time uniform in a custom procedural shader, not by regenerating.
- Phone note: generation is a one-time GPU cost at load. Runtime cost is that of a normal texture sample. Do not regenerate per frame.

### 3.6 Node Material and the Node Material Editor, NME (authored effects)
- URL: https://doc.babylonjs.com/features/featuresDeepDive/materials/node_material/
- Licence in plain words: part of Babylon.js, Apache License 2.0. Free, commercial use allowed.
- What it does: node materials assemble vertex and pixel shader behaviour from blocks, can reuse engine lighting, fog, and bones blocks, and the docs tree includes node material particle shaders and procedural textures. Graphs can be authored visually in the NME, saved as JSON, and loaded at runtime.
- Runtime loading, verified: NodeMaterial.ParseFromSnippetAsync(id, scene) fetches a graph from the Babylon snippet server by immutable id (example id 2F999G in the docs coverage), and ParseFromFileAsync loads JSON you host yourself (webcodingcenter.com NME coverage, found in search results). Measured snippet round trips in that coverage ran 0.36 to 1.3 seconds, so snippets are for prototyping; production should self-host the JSON or build graphs in code.
- Implementation sketches:
  - Shield hit ripple: a node material on a slightly inflated hull shell (or a sphere segment for capital ships). Inputs: hit position as a Vector3 uniform, hit time. Graph: distance from fragment world position to hit position, minus (time since hit times speed), passed through a smoothstep band, multiplied by fresnel, output as emissive in the shield colour (cyan, per the art direction colour law). Fade the band alpha out over about 0.6 seconds. One material instance per ship, two uniforms updated per hit.
  - Engine glow falloff: fresnel-driven emissive on a cone or disc at each exhaust, intensity uniform tied to throttle or movement state. No texture needed.
  - Trail ribbons: the Babylon trail effect walkthrough (babylonjs.medium.com, found in search results) builds trails in the NME by sampling a curve texture twice with a small V offset (-0.001) to derive direction, computing a camera-facing bitangent from the cross product of view direction and curve direction, and shaping alpha with a sine across width times a sine along length. That graph is a complete starting point for projectile and missile trails (Section 7.4).
- Phone note: node materials compile to the same shaders as hand-written ones. Cost is the fragment work you build. Keep ripple and glow graphs short; avoid per-pixel texture samples beyond one or two.

### 3.7 ShaderMaterial (full custom GLSL or WGSL)
- URL: https://doc.babylonjs.com/features/featuresDeepDive/materials/shaders/shaderMaterial/
- Licence in plain words: part of Babylon.js, Apache 2.0.
- What it does, verified from the docs page opened in research: ShaderMaterial passes data from the scene to your vertex and fragment shaders and applies the result to any mesh. Shader code can be supplied via the ShadersStore, script elements, source strings, or external .vertex.fx and .fragment.fx files. Attributes and uniforms are declared in option arrays; any attribute used must be listed, and worldViewProjection must be declared if used. Texture uniforms go in the samplers array and are set with setTexture; scalars and vectors with setFloat and setVector3.
- Use for: shockwave rings (Section 7.3), charge-up distortions, and any effect the node system expresses awkwardly.
- Image processing warning, verified: if an ImageProcessingPostProcess or DefaultRenderingPipeline is active, a custom shader outputting gl_FragColor will look brighter than intended unless the shader includes the imageProcessingCompatibility include or the material toggles the IMAGEPROCESSINGPOSTPROCESS define (docs page: https://doc.babylonjs.com/features/featuresDeepDive/materials/shaders/image_processing). Budget ten minutes for this the first time a custom shader meets the pipeline, because everyone hits it once.
- Phone note: a fullscreen ShaderMaterial post effect costs a full-screen pass. Confine custom shaders to meshes (rings, shells, cones) wherever possible.

### 3.8 DefaultRenderingPipeline (the post backbone)
- URL: https://doc.babylonjs.com/features/featuresDeepDive/postProcesses/defaultRenderingPipeline/
- Licence in plain words: part of Babylon.js, Apache 2.0.
- Verified feature set, from the docs page opened in research: constructed as new DefaultRenderingPipeline(name, hdr, scene, cameras). Properties include samples (MSAA sample count; 1 by default), imageProcessing (contrast, exposure, colour grading via a .3dl ColorGradingTexture or a PNG, and ColorCurves with global, highlights, midtones, and shadows density, exposure, hue, and saturation), bloom (bloomEnabled, bloomKernel 64, bloomScale 0.5, bloomThreshold 0.9, bloomWeight 0.15), chromaticAberration (aberrationAmount 30 by default), depthOfField (fStop 1.4, focalLength 50, focusDistance 2000, lensSize 50), fxaa, glowLayer (blurKernelSize 16, intensity 1), grain (intensity 30, animated flag), and sharpen (edgeAmount 0.3, colorAmount 1).
- Phone-critical note, verified in forum evidence during research: with HDR on, set pipeline.samples (for example 4) or edges suffer, because the HDR path renders into offscreen targets where default MSAA does not apply.
